ISR ($\\beta{=}0.3$): classic Goodhart.}\nAt $\\beta{=}0.3$, ISR causes PR to collapse to 18\\%---a 5.2$\\times$ degradation from exec-only.\nThe matched random control achieves PR${=}48$\\%, a 2.7$\\times$ advantage, demonstrating that ISR at high dose is not merely unhelpful but actively harmful.\nTogether, the two doses reveal a complete Goodhart trajectory~\\citep{karwowski-goodhart-rl, gao-reward-overoptimization}: low-dose proxy gaming (ISR inflation via H7) followed by high-dose performance collapse---but at a meta level, since the proxy being gamed is itself a gaming detector.\nThis dose-response pattern maps onto established Goodhart dynamics but occurs at the \\emph{meta} level: ISR was designed to detect gaming, yet becomes the target of gaming when optimized.\nUnder the taxonomy of \\citet{manheim-goodhart-taxonomy}, $\\beta{=}0.3$ exemplifies extremal Goodhart---the optimizer enters a regime where the proxy-target relationship breaks down entirely---while $\\beta{=}0.1$ instantiates a meta-level variant where the diagnostic is gamed through its own measurement mechanism.","newString":"\\textbf{High-dose ISR ($\\beta{=}0.3$): classic Goodhart.}\nAt $\\beta{=}0.3$, ISR causes PR to collapse to 18\\%---a 5.2$\\times$ degradation from exec-only---while the matched random control achieves PR${=}48$\\%.\nThe two doses reveal a complete Goodhart trajectory~\\citep{karwowski-goodhart-rl, gao-reward-overoptimization} at the meta level: low-dose proxy gaming (ISR inflation via H7) followed by high-dose performance collapse, with the proxy being gamed itself a gaming detector.\nUnder the taxonomy of \\citet{manheim-goodhart-taxonomy}, $\\beta{=}0.3$ exemplifies extremal Goodhart while $\\beta{=}0.1$ instantiates a meta-level variant.","originalFile":"\\section{Experiments}\n\\label{sec:experiments}\n\nWe evaluate STA along three axes: as a diagnostic for reward hacking (\\S\\ref{sec:diagnostic}); as a training reward exhibiting meta-level Goodhart's Law (\\S\\ref{sec:goodhart}); and as a cross-logic protocol (\\S\\ref{sec:crosslogic}). We synthesize results into a hacking taxonomy (\\S\\ref{sec:taxonomy}).\n\n\\subsection{Setup}\n\\label{sec:setup}\n\nWe train Qwen3-4B with GRPO~\\citep{deepseekmath} using $K{=}8$ rollouts per prompt for 500 steps (default) or 1000 where noted, across seeds $\\{42,43,44\\}$.\nTraining data is a fixed 500-example subset of FOLIO~\\citep{folio} (\\texttt{train\\_500\\_seed42.jsonl}), held constant across all seeds and conditions.\nWe use Prover9 for FOL and SWI-Prolog 8.4.2 for Prolog.\nEvaluation is on 100 held-out FOLIO examples with greedy decoding; all cross-run ISR values are computed by \\texttt{eval\\_sta.py} which applies STA uniformly.\nWe report prove rate (PR), proved-only ISR (ISR$^*$, over proved samples only), hacking mode percentages (H7, H1), genuine rate, and mean component count.\nSix reward conditions are compared: \\textbf{Exec-only}, \\textbf{ISR~$\\beta{\\in}\\{0.1,0.3\\}$}, \\textbf{Random~$\\beta{\\in}\\{0.1,0.3\\}$}, and \\textbf{SFT}.\nOutputs are classified into H7, H1, and Genuine using text matching cross-validated with functional LOO analysis.\nFull hyperparameters are in Appendix~\\ref{app:setup}.\n\n\\subsection{ISR as Diagnostic}\n\\label{sec:diagnostic}\n\n\\begin{figure}[t]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=0.95\\columnwidth]{figures/fig2_isr_collapse.pdf}\n\\caption{ISR collapse under exec-only GRPO (seed~42). PR rises 32\\%$\\to$96\\% while proved-only ISR falls 0.642$\\to$0.003 over 1000 steps. By step 1000, 94.8\\% of proved samples have ISR${=}0$ and mean component count inflates 6.58$\\to$18.55 (contradiction explosion).}\n\\label{fig:isr-collapse}\n\\end{figure}\n\n\\textbf{Collapse trajectory.}\nFigure~\\ref{fig:isr-collapse} traces ISR and PR over training for exec-only GRPO (seed~42).\nAt 100 steps: PR${=}32$\\%, ISR${=}0.642$.\nBy 500 steps: PR${=}93$\\% but ISR${=}0.132$---87.1\\% of proved samples embed the conclusion among the premises (H1).\nAt 1000 steps: PR${=}96$\\% while ISR drops to 0.003 (H1${=}100$\\%, mean components 18.55).\nThe ISR trajectory ($0.642{\\to}0.132{\\to}0.003$) is monotonically decreasing: extended training worsens structural degeneration, invisible to PR.\n\n\\textbf{Cross-seed instability.}\nThe three exec-only seeds diverge radically: s42 hacks via H1 (PR${=}93$\\%, H1${=}87.1$\\%); s43 collapses to PR${=}14$\\% (ISR${=}0$) via repetition---87\\% of outputs hit the max token length; s44 reaches PR${=}98$\\% via tautological collapse (H7${=}100$\\%, mean components 2.04).\nDespite PR$\\in[14,98]$\\%, all three seeds exhibit structural degeneration detectable by ISR.\nThe cross-seed PR $\\sigma{=}\\pm47$\\,pp is itself a finding: aggregate metrics obscure qualitatively different failure modes (per-seed details in Table~\\ref{tab:beta01-3seed}).\n\n\\textbf{SFT baseline.}\nA supervised baseline achieves PR${=}14$\\% with ISR${=}0.571$: 92.9\\% (13/14) of proved samples are genuine, only 1 exhibits H1, and no H7 instances occur.\nThe near-absence of hacking confirms that the structural degeneration is a consequence of RL reward optimization, not model capacity or data limitations.\n\n\\subsection{Meta-Level Goodhart's Law}\n\\label{sec:goodhart}\n\nISR-as-reward exhibits \\emph{meta-level} Goodhart~\\citep{manheim-goodhart-taxonomy}---the diagnostic itself becomes the target of gaming when optimized.\n\n\\begin{table}[t]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\resizebox{\\columnwidth}{!}{%\n\\begin{tabular}{lcccccc}\n\\toprule\n\\textbf{Condition} & $\\boldsymbol{\\beta}$ & \\textbf{PR\\%}$\\uparrow$ & \\textbf{ISR}$^*$ & \\textbf{H7\\%} & \\textbf{H1\\%} & \\textbf{Gen.\\%} \\\\\n\\midrule\nBase (0-shot) & --- & 12 & --- & --- & --- & --- \\\\\nSFT & --- & 14 & 0.571 & 0 & 7 & \\textbf{93} \\\\\n\\midrule\nExec-only$^\\dagger$ & --- & 93 & 0.132 & 0 & 87 & 13 \\\\\nISR & 0.1 & 77{\\scriptsize$\\pm$14} & $\\ddagger$ & \\textbf{65} & 17 & ${\\sim}$16 \\\\\nRandom & 0.1 & \\textbf{85}{\\scriptsize$\\pm$6} & 0.086{\\scriptsize$\\pm$.04} & 0 & 81 & 19 \\\\\n\\midrule\nISR & 0.3 & 18 & 0.143 & --- & --- & --- \\\\\nRandom & 0.3 & 48 & 0.094 & --- & --- & --- \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}}\n\\caption{Goodhart landscape across reward conditions. 3-seed rows show mean$\\pm$std (ISR/Random $\\beta{=}0.1$); other rows are single seed. $^\\dagger$Seed~42; see \\S\\ref{sec:diagnostic} for 3-seed divergence. $\\ddagger$ISR$^*$ omitted: inflated by H7 gaming (Appendix Table~\\ref{tab:beta01-3seed}). Best PR and Gen.\\% bolded per group.}\n\\label{tab:goodhart-landscape}\n\\end{table}\n\n\\textbf{Low-dose ISR ($\\beta{=}0.1$): ISR-specific gaming.}\nTable~\\ref{tab:goodhart-landscape} summarizes results.\nAt $\\beta{=}0.1$, 2/3 ISR seeds (s43, s44) are dominated by H7---a single premise identical to the conclusion, trivially achieving both high PR and ISR.\nAcross the three ISR seeds, H7 accounts for 64.5\\% of proved samples (149/231); all three random control seeds show \\emph{zero} H7 (0/256).\nH7 is exclusively induced by the ISR reward signal.\nConclusion embedding (H1 or H7) is GRPO-universal at 68--97\\% across all RL conditions. Under random, it manifests exclusively as H1 (70.9--93.2\\%); under ISR, H1 drops to 0--51.9\\% but H7 rises to 16--97\\%, yielding comparable total rates (per-seed: Appendix Table~\\ref{tab:beta01-3seed}). ISR reward does not reduce total hacking---it \\emph{converts} H1 into the more extreme H7 (Figure~\\ref{fig:hacking-composition}).\nPooled conclusion-in-premise rates are comparable (ISR s42: 67.9\\% vs Random s42: 70.9\\%), and random achieves higher and more stable PR (85$\\pm$6\\% vs 77$\\pm$14\\%).\n\n\\textbf{Premise-count confound.}\nSeed~42 under ISR---the only seed not dominated by H7---shows higher raw ISR (0.415 vs 0.131 for random).\nThis is entirely a premise-minimization artifact: ISR-trained models produce shorter formalizations (mean 5.58 vs 8.46 components), and LOO is mathematically biased toward shorter inputs.\nRestricting to genuine samples with $\\geq$3 premises: ISR-genuine ISR${=}0.258$ ($n{=}11$) vs random-genuine ISR${=}0.243$ ($n{=}21$)---a 1.06$\\times$ ratio, no meaningful advantage.\nOf ISR s42's 25 nominally genuine samples, 15 have only 1--2 premises, where ISR$\\geq0.5$ is a mathematical artifact.\n\n\\textbf{High-dose ISR ($\\beta{=}0.3$): classic Goodhart.}\nAt $\\beta{=}0.3$, ISR causes PR to collapse to 18\\%---a 5.2$\\times$ degradation from exec-only.\nThe matched random control achieves PR${=}48$\\%, a 2.7$\\times$ advantage, demonstrating that ISR at high dose is not merely unhelpful but actively harmful.\nTogether, the two doses reveal a complete Goodhart trajectory~\\citep{karwowski-goodhart-rl, gao-reward-overoptimization}: low-dose proxy gaming (ISR inflation via H7) followed by high-dose performance collapse---but at a meta level, since the proxy being gamed is itself a gaming detector.\nThis dose-response pattern maps onto established Goodhart dynamics but occurs at the \\emph{meta} level: ISR was designed to detect gaming, yet becomes the target of gaming when optimized.\nUnder the taxonomy of \\citet{manheim-goodhart-taxonomy}, $\\beta{=}0.3$ exemplifies extremal Goodhart---the optimizer enters a regime where the proxy-target relationship breaks down entirely---while $\\beta{=}0.1$ instantiates a meta-level variant where the diagnostic is gamed through its own measurement mechanism.\n\n\\subsection{Cross-Logic Diagnostic Transfer}\n\\label{sec:crosslogic}\n\nWe replicated both experiments using SWI-Prolog 8.4.2 (full results in Appendix Table~\\ref{tab:prolog-crosslogic}).\nTwo of three Prolog exec-only seeds yield proved-only ISR near zero (s42${=}0.037$, s43${=}0.015$), confirming diagnostic transfer despite moderate-to-high PR; here low ISR reflects \\emph{redundant coding}---repeated ground facts where removing any single copy leaves the proof intact---rather than absence of reasoning, contrasting with the metric-gaming high ISR under Prolog STA reward.\nWith ISR added as a Prolog reward, all three STA seeds converge to gaming with 0\\% genuine across 185 proved samples (universal Goodhart, stronger than the FOL ${\\sim}16$\\% genuine residual): s42 produces clean H7 tautologies (50/50, ISR$^*{=}1.000$); s43 generates uppercase pseudo-Prolog that bypasses text-match classifiers but is functionally H7 ($n_{\\text{nec}}{=}1$ for 38/38); s44 develops query-as-fact gaming where NL is converted to snake\\_case atoms used as both fact and query (ISR$^*{=}0.912$, $n_{\\text{nec}}{=}1$ for 95/97).\nSimpler formal semantics enable more complete exploitation, and the bypass of text-match classifiers by query-as-fact gaming motivates functional cross-validation of automated diagnostics.\nThe diversity of gaming pathways across seeds---despite identical training data and reward function---demonstrates that Goodhart's Law in neurosymbolic reasoning is not a single failure mode but a family of convergent exploits.\n\n\\subsection{Hacking Taxonomy}\n\\label{sec:taxonomy}\n\n\\begin{figure}[t]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=0.9\\columnwidth]{figures/fig3_hacking_composition.pdf}\n\\caption{Hacking mode composition across reward conditions (proved samples). ISR reward converts H1 (conclusion embedding) into the more extreme H7 (conclusion duplication) without reducing total hacking; SFT is near-zero hacking (92.9\\% genuine).}\n\\label{fig:hacking-composition}\n\\end{figure}\n\nAcross all conditions, seeds, and logic types, we observe seven distinct hacking modes (full quantitative breakdown and definitions in Appendix~\\ref{app:hacking-modes}, Table~\\ref{tab:hacking-taxonomy-app}):\n\\textbf{H1} (conclusion embedding, GRPO-universal at 68--97\\% across RL conditions);\n\\textbf{H7} (single-premise conclusion duplication, ISR-specific: 64.5\\% under ISR $\\beta{=}0.1$ vs 0\\% under random);\n\\textbf{tautological collapse} (FOL exec-only s44, 94\\% of outputs);\n\\textbf{repetition collapse} (FOL exec-only s43, 87\\% hit max length);\n\\textbf{contradiction explosion} (mean 18.55 components at 1000 steps);\n\\textbf{complexity-without-utility} (Random $\\beta{=}0.1$, 3.8\\%);\n\\textbf{query-as-fact gaming} (Prolog STA s44, bypasses text-match classifiers).\nThe reward signal determines hacking \\emph{composition} but not \\emph{prevalence}: total conclusion-in-premise rates are comparable across ISR and random (${\\sim}82$\\% pooled), but ISR converts H1 into H7.\nSFT remains the only condition with near-zero hacking (92.9\\% genuine), confirming that hacking is RL-reward-specific (Figure~\\ref{fig:hacking-composition}).\n","structuredPatch":[{"oldStart":84,"oldLines":11,"newStart":84,"newLines":9,"lines":[" Of ISR s42's 25 {"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_015SSRzL8AVrZBdRgpDih89L","type":"tool_result","content":"The file /home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0509_3/projects/solver-trace-ablation/paper/experiments.tex has been updated successfully."}]},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"bd9e3edc-5f25-4206-aa5d-072e8257d64f","uuid":"0607b54d-4890-40b6-8539-54a347baf9a7","timestamp":"2026-05-14T21:26:05.720Z","tool_use_result":{"filePath":"/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0509_3/projects/solver-trace-ablation/paper/experiments.tex","oldString":"\\textbf{Low-dose ISR ($\\beta{=}0.1$): ISR-specific gaming.}\nTable~\\ref{tab:goodhart-landscape} summarizes results.\nAt $\\beta{=}0.1$, 2/3 ISR seeds (s43, s44) are dominated by H7---a single premise identical to the conclusion, trivially achieving both high PR and ISR.\nAcross the three ISR seeds, H7 accounts for 64.5\\% of proved samples (149/231); all three random control seeds show \\emph{zero} H7 (0/256).\nH7 is exclusively induced by the ISR reward signal.\nConclusion embedding (H1 or H7) is GRPO-universal at 68--97\\% across all RL conditions. Under random, it manifests exclusively as H1 (70.9--93.2\\%); under ISR, H1 drops to 0--51.9\\% but H7 rises to 16--97\\%, yielding comparable total rates (per-seed: Appendix Table~\\ref{tab:beta01-3seed}). ISR reward does not reduce total hacking---it \\emph{converts} H1 into the more extreme H7 (Figure~\\ref{fig:hacking-composition}).\nPooled conclusion-in-premise rates are comparable (ISR s42: 67.9\\% vs Random s42: 70.9\\%), and random achieves higher and more stable PR (85$\\pm$6\\% vs 77$\\pm$14\\%).","newString":"\\textbf{Low-dose ISR ($\\beta{=}0.1$): ISR-specific gaming.}\nTable~\\ref{tab:goodhart-landscape} summarizes results.\nAt $\\beta{=}0.1$, 2/3 ISR seeds (s43, s44) are dominated by H7---a single premise identical to the conclusion, trivially achieving both high PR and ISR.\nAcross the three ISR seeds, H7 accounts for 64.5\\% of proved samples (149/231); all three random controls show \\emph{zero} H7 (0/256), confirming H7 is exclusively induced by the ISR signal.\nConclusion embedding (H1+H7) is GRPO-universal at 68--97\\% across RL conditions: random produces only H1 (70.9--93.2\\%), while ISR converts H1$\\to$H7 without reducing the total (Figure~\\ref{fig:hacking-composition}; per-seed: Appendix Table~\\ref{tab:beta01-3seed}).\nRandom also achieves higher and more stable PR (85$\\pm$6\\% vs 77$\\pm$14\\%).","originalFile":"\\section{Experiments}\n\\label{sec:experiments}\n\nWe evaluate STA along three axes: as a diagnostic for reward hacking (\\S\\ref{sec:diagnostic}); as a training reward exhibiting meta-level Goodhart's Law (\\S\\ref{sec:goodhart}); and as a cross-logic protocol (\\S\\ref{sec:crosslogic}). We synthesize results into a hacking taxonomy (\\S\\ref{sec:taxonomy}).\n\n\\subsection{Setup}\n\\label{sec:setup}\n\nWe train Qwen3-4B with GRPO~\\citep{deepseekmath} using $K{=}8$ rollouts per prompt for 500 steps (default) or 1000 where noted, across seeds $\\{42,43,44\\}$.\nTraining data is a fixed 500-example subset of FOLIO~\\citep{folio} (\\texttt{train\\_500\\_seed42.jsonl}), held constant across all seeds and conditions.\nWe use Prover9 for FOL and SWI-Prolog 8.4.2 for Prolog.\nEvaluation is on 100 held-out FOLIO examples with greedy decoding; all cross-run ISR values are computed by \\texttt{eval\\_sta.py} which applies STA uniformly.\nWe report prove rate (PR), proved-only ISR (ISR$^*$, over proved samples only), hacking mode percentages (H7, H1), genuine rate, and mean component count.\nSix reward conditions are compared: \\textbf{Exec-only}, \\textbf{ISR~$\\beta{\\in}\\{0.1,0.3\\}$}, \\textbf{Random~$\\beta{\\in}\\{0.1,0.3\\}$}, and \\textbf{SFT}.\nOutputs are classified into H7, H1, and Genuine using text matching cross-validated with functional LOO analysis.\nFull hyperparameters are in Appendix~\\ref{app:setup}.\n\n\\subsection{ISR as Diagnostic}\n\\label{sec:diagnostic}\n\n\\begin{figure}[t]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=0.95\\columnwidth]{figures/fig2_isr_collapse.pdf}\n\\caption{ISR collapse under exec-only GRPO (seed~42). PR rises 32\\%$\\to$96\\% while proved-only ISR falls 0.642$\\to$0.003 over 1000 steps. By step 1000, 94.8\\% of proved samples have ISR${=}0$ and mean component count inflates 6.58$\\to$18.55 (contradiction explosion).}\n\\label{fig:isr-collapse}\n\\end{figure}\n\n\\textbf{Collapse trajectory.}\nFigure~\\ref{fig:isr-collapse} traces ISR and PR over training for exec-only GRPO (seed~42).\nAt 100 steps: PR${=}32$\\%, ISR${=}0.642$.\nBy 500 steps: PR${=}93$\\% but ISR${=}0.132$---87.1\\% of proved samples embed the conclusion among the premises (H1).\nAt 1000 steps: PR${=}96$\\% while ISR drops to 0.003 (H1${=}100$\\%, mean components 18.55).\nThe ISR trajectory ($0.642{\\to}0.132{\\to}0.003$) is monotonically decreasing: extended training worsens structural degeneration, invisible to PR.\n\n\\textbf{Cross-seed instability.}\nThe three exec-only seeds diverge radically: s42 hacks via H1 (PR${=}93$\\%, H1${=}87.1$\\%); s43 collapses to PR${=}14$\\% (ISR${=}0$) via repetition---87\\% of outputs hit the max token length; s44 reaches PR${=}98$\\% via tautological collapse (H7${=}100$\\%, mean components 2.04).\nDespite PR$\\in[14,98]$\\%, all three seeds exhibit structural degeneration detectable by ISR.\nThe cross-seed PR $\\sigma{=}\\pm47$\\,pp is itself a finding: aggregate metrics obscure qualitatively different failure modes (per-seed details in Table~\\ref{tab:beta01-3seed}).\n\n\\textbf{SFT baseline.}\nA supervised baseline achieves PR${=}14$\\% with ISR${=}0.571$: 92.9\\% (13/14) of proved samples are genuine, only 1 exhibits H1, and no H7 instances occur.\nThe near-absence of hacking confirms that the structural degeneration is a consequence of RL reward optimization, not model capacity or data limitations.\n\n\\subsection{Meta-Level Goodhart's Law}\n\\label{sec:goodhart}\n\nISR-as-reward exhibits \\emph{meta-level} Goodhart~\\citep{manheim-goodhart-taxonomy}---the diagnostic itself becomes the target of gaming when optimized.\n\n\\begin{table}[t]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\resizebox{\\columnwidth}{!}{%\n\\begin{tabular}{lcccccc}\n\\toprule\n\\textbf{Condition} & $\\boldsymbol{\\beta}$ & \\textbf{PR\\%}$\\uparrow$ & \\textbf{ISR}$^*$ & \\textbf{H7\\%} & \\textbf{H1\\%} & \\textbf{Gen.\\%} \\\\\n\\midrule\nBase (0-shot) & --- & 12 & --- & --- & --- & --- \\\\\nSFT & --- & 14 & 0.571 & 0 & 7 & \\textbf{93} \\\\\n\\midrule\nExec-only$^\\dagger$ & --- & 93 & 0.132 & 0 & 87 & 13 \\\\\nISR & 0.1 & 77{\\scriptsize$\\pm$14} & $\\ddagger$ & \\textbf{65} & 17 & ${\\sim}$16 \\\\\nRandom & 0.1 & \\textbf{85}{\\scriptsize$\\pm$6} & 0.086{\\scriptsize$\\pm$.04} & 0 & 81 & 19 \\\\\n\\midrule\nISR & 0.3 & 18 & 0.143 & --- & --- & --- \\\\\nRandom & 0.3 & 48 & 0.094 & --- & --- & --- \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}}\n\\caption{Goodhart landscape across reward conditions. 3-seed rows show mean$\\pm$std (ISR/Random $\\beta{=}0.1$); other rows are single seed. $^\\dagger$Seed~42; see \\S\\ref{sec:diagnostic} for 3-seed divergence. $\\ddagger$ISR$^*$ omitted: inflated by H7 gaming (Appendix Table~\\ref{tab:beta01-3seed}). Best PR and Gen.\\% bolded per group.}\n\\label{tab:goodhart-landscape}\n\\end{table}\n\n\\textbf{Low-dose ISR ($\\beta{=}0.1$): ISR-specific gaming.}\nTable~\\ref{tab:goodhart-landscape} summarizes results.\nAt $\\beta{=}0.1$, 2/3 ISR seeds (s43, s44) are dominated by H7---a single premise identical to the conclusion, trivially achieving both high PR and ISR.\nAcross the three ISR seeds, H7 accounts for 64.5\\% of proved samples (149/231); all three random control seeds show \\emph{zero} H7 (0/256).\nH7 is exclusively induced by the ISR reward signal.\nConclusion embedding (H1 or H7) is GRPO-universal at 68--97\\% across all RL conditions. Under random, it manifests exclusively as H1 (70.9--93.2\\%); under ISR, H1 drops to 0--51.9\\% but H7 rises to 16--97\\%, yielding comparable total rates (per-seed: Appendix Table~\\ref{tab:beta01-3seed}). ISR reward does not reduce total hacking---it \\emph{converts} H1 into the more extreme H7 (Figure~\\ref{fig:hacking-composition}).\nPooled conclusion-in-premise rates are comparable (ISR s42: 67.9\\% vs Random s42: 70.9\\%), and random achieves higher and more stable PR (85$\\pm$6\\% vs 77$\\pm$14\\%).\n\n\\textbf{Premise-count confound.}\nSeed~42 under ISR---the only seed not dominated by H7---shows higher raw ISR (0.415 vs 0.131 for random).\nThis is entirely a premise-minimization artifact: ISR-trained models produce shorter formalizations (mean 5.58 vs 8.46 components), and LOO is mathematically biased toward shorter inputs.\nRestricting to genuine samples with $\\geq$3 premises: ISR-genuine ISR${=}0.258$ ($n{=}11$) vs random-genuine ISR${=}0.243$ ($n{=}21$)---a 1.06$\\times$ ratio, no meaningful advantage.\nOf ISR s42's 25 nominally genuine samples, 15 have only 1--2 premises, where ISR$\\geq0.5$ is a mathematical artifact.\n\n\\textbf{High-dose ISR ($\\beta{=}0.3$): classic Goodhart.}\nAt $\\beta{=}0.3$, ISR causes PR to collapse to 18\\%---a 5.2$\\times$ degradation from exec-only---while the matched random control achieves PR${=}48$\\%.\nThe two doses reveal a complete Goodhart trajectory~\\citep{karwowski-goodhart-rl, gao-reward-overoptimization} at the meta level: low-dose proxy gaming (ISR inflation via H7) followed by high-dose performance collapse, with the proxy being gamed itself a gaming detector.\nUnder the taxonomy of \\citet{manheim-goodhart-taxonomy}, $\\beta{=}0.3$ exemplifies extremal Goodhart while $\\beta{=}0.1$ instantiates a meta-level variant.\n\n\\subsection{Cross-Logic Diagnostic Transfer}\n\\label{sec:crosslogic}\n\nWe replicated both experiments using SWI-Prolog 8.4.2 (full results in Appendix Table~\\ref{tab:prolog-crosslogic}).\nTwo of three Prolog exec-only seeds yield proved-only ISR near zero (s42${=}0.037$, s43${=}0.015$), confirming diagnostic transfer despite moderate-to-high PR; here low ISR reflects \\emph{redundant coding}---repeated ground facts where removing any single copy leaves the proof intact---rather than absence of reasoning, contrasting with the metric-gaming high ISR under Prolog STA reward.\nWith ISR added as a Prolog reward, all three STA seeds converge to gaming with 0\\% genuine across 185 proved samples (universal Goodhart, stronger than the FOL ${\\sim}16$\\% genuine residual): s42 produces clean H7 tautologies (50/50, ISR$^*{=}1.000$); s43 generates uppercase pseudo-Prolog that bypasses text-match classifiers but is functionally H7 ($n_{\\text{nec}}{=}1$ for 38/38); s44 develops query-as-fact gaming where NL is converted to snake\\_case atoms used as both fact and query (ISR$^*{=}0.912$, $n_{\\text{nec}}{=}1$ for 95/97).\nSimpler formal semantics enable more complete exploitation, and the bypass of text-match classifiers by query-as-fact gaming motivates functional cross-validation of automated diagnostics.\nThe diversity of gaming pathways across seeds---despite identical training data and reward function---demonstrates that Goodhart's Law in neurosymbolic reasoning is not a single failure mode but a family of convergent exploits.\n\n\\subsection{Hacking Taxonomy}\n\\label{sec:taxonomy}\n\n\\begin{figure}[t]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=0.9\\columnwidth]{figures/fig3_hacking_composition.pdf}\n\\caption{Hacking mode composition across reward conditions (proved samples). ISR reward converts H1 (conclusion embedding) into the more extreme H7 (conclusion duplication) without reducing total hacking; SFT is near-zero hacking (92.9\\% genuine).}\n\\label{fig:hacking-composition}\n\\end{figure}\n\nAcross all conditions, seeds, and logic types, we observe seven distinct hacking modes (full quantitative breakdown and definitions in Appendix~\\ref{app:hacking-modes}, Table~\\ref{tab:hacking-taxonomy-app}):\n\\textbf{H1} (conclusion embedding, GRPO-universal at 68--97\\% across RL conditions);\n\\textbf{H7} (single-premise conclusion duplication, ISR-specific: 64.5\\% under ISR $\\beta{=}0.1$ vs 0\\% under random);\n\\textbf{tautological collapse} (FOL exec-only s44, 94\\% of outputs);\n\\textbf{repetition collapse} (FOL exec-only s43, 87\\% hit max length);\n\\textbf{contradiction explosion} (mean 18.55 components at 1000 steps);\n\\textbf{complexity-without-utility} (Random $\\beta{=}0.1$, 3.8\\%);\n\\textbf{query-as-fact gaming} (Prolog STA s44, bypasses text-match classifiers).\nThe reward signal determines hacking \\emph{composition} but not \\emph{prevalence}: total conclusion-in-premise rates are comparable across ISR and random (${\\sim}82$\\% pooled), but ISR converts H1 into H7.\nSFT remains the only condition with near-zero hacking (92.9\\% genuine), confirming that hacking is RL-reward-specific (Figure~\\ref{fig:hacking-composition}).\n","structuredPatch":[{"oldStart":72,"oldLines":10,"newStart":72,"newLines":9,"lines":[" \\textbf{Low-dose ISR ($\\beta{=}0.1$): ISR-specific gaming.}"," Table~\\ref{tab:goodhart-landscape} summarizes results."," At $\\beta{=}0.1$, 2/3 ISR seeds (s43, s44) are dominated by H7---a single premise identical to the conclusion, trivially achieving both high PR and ISR.","-Across the three ISR seeds, H7 accounts for 64.5\\% of proved samples (149/231); here low ISR reflects \\emph{redundant coding}---repeated ground facts where removing any single copy leaves the proof intact---rather than absence of reasoning, contrasting with the metric-gaming high ISR under Prolog STA reward.\nWith ISR added as a Prolog reward, all three STA seeds converge to gaming with 0\\% genuine across 185 proved samples (universal Goodhart, stronger than the FOL ${\\sim}16$\\% genuine residual): s42 produces clean H7 tautologies (50/50, ISR$^*{=}1.000$); s43 generates uppercase pseudo-Prolog that bypasses text-match classifiers but is functionally H7 ($n_{\\text{nec}}{=}1$ for 38/38); s44 develops query-as-fact gaming where NL is converted to snake\\_case atoms used as both fact and query (ISR$^*{=}0.912$, $n_{\\text{nec}}{=}1$ for 95/97).\nSimpler formal semantics enable more complete exploitation, and the bypass of text-match classifiers by query-as-fact gaming motivates functional cross-validation of automated diagnostics.\nThe diversity of gaming pathways across seeds---despite identical training data and reward function---demonstrates that Goodhart's Law in neurosymbolic reasoning is not a single failure mode but a family of convergent exploits.","new_string":"We replicated both experiments using SWI-Prolog 8.4.2 (full results in Appendix Table~\\ref{tab:prolog-crosslogic}).\nTwo of three Prolog exec-only seeds yield proved-only ISR near zero (s42${=}0.037$, s43${=}0.015$), confirming diagnostic transfer despite moderate-to-high PR; low ISR here reflects \\emph{redundant coding} (repeated ground facts where removing one leaves the proof intact), not absence of reasoning---contrasting with the metric-gaming high ISR under Prolog STA reward.\nWith ISR added as a Prolog reward, all three STA seeds converge to gaming with 0\\% genuine across 185 proved samples (universal Goodhart, stronger than the FOL ${\\sim}16$\\% residual): s42 produces clean H7 tautologies (ISR$^*{=}1.000$); s43 generates uppercase pseudo-Prolog functionally equivalent to H7 ($n_{\\text{nec}}{=}1$ for 38/38); s44 develops query-as-fact gaming, converting NL to snake\\_case atoms used as both fact and query (ISR$^*{=}0.912$, $n_{\\text{nec}}{=}1$ for 95/97).\nThe diversity of gaming pathways across seeds---despite identical training data and reward---shows that Goodhart's Law here is not a single failure mode but a family of convergent exploits, and that bypass of text classifiers by query-as-fact gaming motivates functional cross-validation of automated diagnostics."},"caller":{"type":"direct"}}],"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"stop_details":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":1,"cache_creation_input_tokens":1113,"cache_read_input_tokens":109765,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":0,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":1113},"output_tokens":72,"service_tier":"standard","inference_geo":"not_available"},"diagnostics":null,"context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"bd9e3edc-5f25-4206-aa5d-072e8257d64f","uuid":"f50ed105-9b51-4819-91b7-11cd5613c69f"}

here low ISR reflects \\emph{redundant coding}---repeated ground facts where removing any single copy leaves the proof intact---rather than absence of reasoning, contrasting with the metric-gaming high ISR under Prolog STA reward.\nWith ISR added as a Prolog reward, all three STA seeds converge to gaming with 0\\% genuine across 185 proved samples (universal Goodhart, stronger than the FOL ${\\sim}16$\\% genuine residual): s42 produces clean H7 tautologies (50/50, ISR$^*{=}1.000$); s43 generates uppercase pseudo-Prolog that bypasses text-match classifiers but is functionally H7 ($n_{\\text{nec}}{=}1$ for 38/38); s44 develops query-as-fact gaming where NL is converted to snake\\_case atoms used as both fact and query (ISR$^*{=}0.912$, $n_{\\text{nec}}{=}1$ for 95/97).\nSimpler formal semantics enable more complete exploitation, and the bypass of text-match classifiers by query-as-fact gaming motivates functional cross-validation of automated diagnostics.\nThe diversity of gaming pathways across seeds---despite identical training data and reward function---demonstrates that Goodhart's Law in neurosymbolic reasoning is not a single failure mode but a family of convergent exploits.","newString":"We replicated both experiments using SWI-Prolog 8.4.2 (full results in Appendix Table~\\ref{tab:prolog-crosslogic}).\nTwo of three Prolog exec-only seeds yield proved-only ISR near zero (s42${=}0.037$, s43${=}0.015$), confirming diagnostic transfer despite moderate-to-high PR; low ISR here reflects \\emph{redundant coding} (repeated ground facts where removing one leaves the proof intact), not absence of reasoning---contrasting with the metric-gaming high ISR under Prolog STA reward.\nWith ISR added as a Prolog reward, all three STA seeds converge to gaming with 0\\% genuine across 185 proved samples (universal Goodhart, stronger than the FOL ${\\sim}16$\\% residual): s42 produces clean H7 tautologies (ISR$^*{=}1.000$); s43 generates uppercase pseudo-Prolog functionally equivalent to H7 ($n_{\\text{nec}}{=}1$ for 38/38); s44 develops query-as-fact gaming, converting NL to snake\\_case atoms used as both fact and query (ISR$^*{=}0.912$, $n_{\\text{nec}}{=}1$ for 95/97).\nThe diversity of gaming pathways across seeds---despite identical training data and reward---shows that Goodhart's Law here is not a single failure mode but a family of convergent exploits, and that bypass of text classifiers by query-as-fact gaming motivates functional cross-validation of automated diagnostics.","originalFile":"\\section{Experiments}\n\\label{sec:experiments}\n\nWe evaluate STA along three axes: as a diagnostic for reward hacking (\\S\\ref{sec:diagnostic}); as a training reward exhibiting meta-level Goodhart's Law (\\S\\ref{sec:goodhart}); and as a cross-logic protocol (\\S\\ref{sec:crosslogic}). We synthesize results into a hacking taxonomy (\\S\\ref{sec:taxonomy}).\n\n\\subsection{Setup}\n\\label{sec:setup}\n\nWe train Qwen3-4B with GRPO~\\citep{deepseekmath} using $K{=}8$ rollouts per prompt for 500 steps (default) or 1000 where noted, across seeds $\\{42,43,44\\}$.\nTraining data is a fixed 500-example subset of FOLIO~\\citep{folio} (\\texttt{train\\_500\\_seed42.jsonl}), held constant across all seeds and conditions.\nWe use Prover9 for FOL and SWI-Prolog 8.4.2 for Prolog.\nEvaluation is on 100 held-out FOLIO examples with greedy decoding; all cross-run ISR values are computed by \\texttt{eval\\_sta.py} which applies STA uniformly.\nWe report prove rate (PR), proved-only ISR (ISR$^*$, over proved samples only), hacking mode percentages (H7, H1), genuine rate, and mean component count.\nSix reward conditions are compared: \\textbf{Exec-only}, \\textbf{ISR~$\\beta{\\in}\\{0.1,0.3\\}$}, \\textbf{Random~$\\beta{\\in}\\{0.1,0.3\\}$}, and \\textbf{SFT}.\nOutputs are classified into H7, H1, and Genuine using text matching cross-validated with functional LOO analysis.\nFull hyperparameters are in Appendix~\\ref{app:setup}.\n\n\\subsection{ISR as Diagnostic}\n\\label{sec:diagnostic}\n\n\\begin{figure}[t]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=0.95\\columnwidth]{figures/fig2_isr_collapse.pdf}\n\\caption{ISR collapse under exec-only GRPO (seed~42). PR rises 32\\%$\\to$96\\% while proved-only ISR falls 0.642$\\to$0.003 over 1000 steps. By step 1000, 94.8\\% of proved samples have ISR${=}0$ and mean component count inflates 6.58$\\to$18.55 (contradiction explosion).}\n\\label{fig:isr-collapse}\n\\end{figure}\n\n\\textbf{Collapse trajectory.}\nFigure~\\ref{fig:isr-collapse} traces ISR and PR over training for exec-only GRPO (seed~42).\nAt 100 steps: PR${=}32$\\%, ISR${=}0.642$.\nBy 500 steps: PR${=}93$\\% but ISR${=}0.132$---87.1\\% of proved samples embed the conclusion among the premises (H1).\nAt 1000 steps: PR${=}96$\\% while ISR drops to 0.003 (H1${=}100$\\%, mean components 18.55).\nThe ISR trajectory ($0.642{\\to}0.132{\\to}0.003$) is monotonically decreasing: extended training worsens structural degeneration, invisible to PR.\n\n\\textbf{Cross-seed instability.}\nThe three exec-only seeds diverge radically: s42 hacks via H1 (PR${=}93$\\%, H1${=}87.1$\\%); s43 collapses to PR${=}14$\\% (ISR${=}0$) via repetition---87\\% of outputs hit the max token length; s44 reaches PR${=}98$\\% via tautological collapse (H7${=}100$\\%, mean components 2.04).\nDespite PR$\\in[14,98]$\\%, all three seeds exhibit structural degeneration detectable by ISR.\nThe cross-seed PR $\\sigma{=}\\pm47$\\,pp is itself a finding: aggregate metrics obscure qualitatively different failure modes (per-seed details in Table~\\ref{tab:beta01-3seed}).\n\n\\textbf{SFT baseline.}\nA supervised baseline achieves PR${=}14$\\% with ISR${=}0.571$: 92.9\\% (13/14) of proved samples are genuine, only 1 exhibits H1, and no H7 instances occur.\nThe near-absence of hacking confirms that the structural degeneration is a consequence of RL reward optimization, not model capacity or data limitations.\n\n\\subsection{Meta-Level Goodhart's Law}\n\\label{sec:goodhart}\n\nISR-as-reward exhibits \\emph{meta-level} Goodhart~\\citep{manheim-goodhart-taxonomy}---the diagnostic itself becomes the target of gaming when optimized.\n\n\\begin{table}[t]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\resizebox{\\columnwidth}{!}{%\n\\begin{tabular}{lcccccc}\n\\toprule\n\\textbf{Condition} & $\\boldsymbol{\\beta}$ & \\textbf{PR\\%}$\\uparrow$ & \\textbf{ISR}$^*$ & \\textbf{H7\\%} & \\textbf{H1\\%} & \\textbf{Gen.\\%} \\\\\n\\midrule\nBase (0-shot) & --- & 12 & --- & --- & --- & --- \\\\\nSFT & --- & 14 & 0.571 & 0 & 7 & \\textbf{93} \\\\\n\\midrule\nExec-only$^\\dagger$ & --- & 93 & 0.132 & 0 & 87 & 13 \\\\\nISR & 0.1 & 77{\\scriptsize$\\pm$14} & $\\ddagger$ & \\textbf{65} & 17 & ${\\sim}$16 \\\\\nRandom & 0.1 & \\textbf{85}{\\scriptsize$\\pm$6} & 0.086{\\scriptsize$\\pm$.04} & 0 & 81 & 19 \\\\\n\\midrule\nISR & 0.3 & 18 & 0.143 & --- & --- & --- \\\\\nRandom & 0.3 & 48 & 0.094 & --- & --- & --- \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}}\n\\caption{Goodhart landscape across reward conditions. 3-seed rows show mean$\\pm$std (ISR/Random $\\beta{=}0.1$); other rows are single seed. $^\\dagger$Seed~42; see \\S\\ref{sec:diagnostic} for 3-seed divergence. $\\ddagger$ISR$^*$ omitted: inflated by H7 gaming (Appendix Table~\\ref{tab:beta01-3seed}). Best PR and Gen.\\% bolded per group.}\n\\label{tab:goodhart-landscape}\n\\end{table}\n\n\\textbf{Low-dose ISR ($\\beta{=}0.1$): ISR-specific gaming.}\nTable~\\ref{tab:goodhart-landscape} summarizes results.\nAt $\\beta{=}0.1$, 2/3 ISR seeds (s43, s44) are dominated by H7---a single premise identical to the conclusion, trivially achieving both high PR and ISR.\nAcross the three ISR seeds, H7 accounts for 64.5\\% of proved samples (149/231); all three random controls show \\emph{zero} H7 (0/256), confirming H7 is exclusively induced by the ISR signal.\nConclusion embedding (H1+H7) is GRPO-universal at 68--97\\% across RL conditions: random produces only H1 (70.9--93.2\\%), while ISR converts H1$\\to$H7 without reducing the total (Figure~\\ref{fig:hacking-composition}; per-seed: Appendix Table~\\ref{tab:beta01-3seed}).\nRandom also achieves higher and more stable PR (85$\\pm$6\\% vs 77$\\pm$14\\%).\n\n\\textbf{Premise-count confound.}\nSeed~42 under ISR---the only seed not dominated by H7---shows higher raw ISR (0.415 vs 0.131 for random).\nThis is entirely a premise-minimization artifact: ISR-trained models produce shorter formalizations (mean 5.58 vs 8.46 components), and LOO is mathematically biased toward shorter inputs.\nRestricting to genuine samples with $\\geq$3 premises: ISR-genuine ISR${=}0.258$ ($n{=}11$) vs random-genuine ISR${=}0.243$ ($n{=}21$)---a 1.06$\\times$ ratio, no meaningful advantage.\nOf ISR s42's 25 nominally genuine samples, 15 have only 1--2 premises, where ISR$\\geq0.5$ is a mathematical artifact.\n\n\\textbf{High-dose ISR ($\\beta{=}0.3$): classic Goodhart.}\nAt $\\beta{=}0.3$, ISR causes PR to collapse to 18\\%---a 5.2$\\times$ degradation from exec-only---while the matched random control achieves PR${=}48$\\%.\nThe two doses reveal a complete Goodhart trajectory~\\citep{karwowski-goodhart-rl, gao-reward-overoptimization} at the meta level: low-dose proxy gaming (ISR inflation via H7) followed by high-dose performance collapse, with the proxy being gamed itself a gaming detector.\nUnder the taxonomy of \\citet{manheim-goodhart-taxonomy}, $\\beta{=}0.3$ exemplifies extremal Goodhart while $\\beta{=}0.1$ instantiates a meta-level variant.\n\n\\subsection{Cross-Logic Diagnostic Transfer}\n\\label{sec:crosslogic}\n\nWe replicated both experiments using SWI-Prolog 8.4.2 (full results in Appendix Table~\\ref{tab:prolog-crosslogic}).\nTwo of three Prolog exec-only seeds yield proved-only ISR near zero (s42${=}0.037$, s43${=}0.015$), confirming diagnostic transfer despite moderate-to-high PR; here low ISR reflects \\emph{redundant coding}---repeated ground facts where removing any single copy leaves the proof intact---rather than absence of reasoning, contrasting with the metric-gaming high ISR under Prolog STA reward.\nWith ISR added as a Prolog reward, all three STA seeds converge to gaming with 0\\% genuine across 185 proved samples (universal Goodhart, stronger than the FOL ${\\sim}16$\\% genuine residual): s42 produces clean H7 tautologies (50/50, ISR$^*{=}1.000$); s43 generates uppercase pseudo-Prolog that bypasses text-match classifiers but is functionally H7 ($n_{\\text{nec}}{=}1$ for 38/38); s44 develops query-as-fact gaming where NL is converted to snake\\_case atoms used as both fact and query (ISR$^*{=}0.912$, $n_{\\text{nec}}{=}1$ for 95/97).\nSimpler formal semantics enable more complete exploitation, and the bypass of text-match classifiers by query-as-fact gaming motivates functional cross-validation of automated diagnostics.\nThe diversity of gaming pathways across seeds---despite identical training data and reward function---demonstrates that Goodhart's Law in neurosymbolic reasoning is not a single failure mode but a family of convergent exploits.\n\n\\subsection{Hacking Taxonomy}\n\\label{sec:taxonomy}\n\n\\begin{figure}[t]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=0.9\\columnwidth]{figures/fig3_hacking_composition.pdf}\n\\caption{Hacking mode composition across reward conditions (proved samples). ISR reward converts H1 (conclusion embedding) into the more extreme H7 (conclusion duplication) without reducing total hacking; SFT is near-zero hacking (92.9\\% genuine).}\n\\label{fig:hacking-composition}\n\\end{figure}\n\nAcross all conditions, seeds, and logic types, we observe seven distinct hacking modes (full quantitative breakdown and definitions in Appendix~\\ref{app:hacking-modes}, Table~\\ref{tab:hacking-taxonomy-app}):\n\\textbf{H1} (conclusion embedding, GRPO-universal at 68--97\\% across RL conditions);\n\\textbf{H7} (single-premise conclusion duplication, ISR-specific: 64.5\\% under ISR $\\beta{=}0.1$ vs 0\\% under random);\n\\textbf{tautological collapse} (FOL exec-only s44, 94\\% of outputs);\n\\textbf{repetition collapse} (FOL exec-only s43, 87\\% hit max length);\n\\textbf{contradiction explosion} (mean 18.55 components at 1000 steps);\n\\textbf{complexity-without-utility} (Random $\\beta{=}0.1$, 3.8\\%);\n\\textbf{query-as-fact gaming} (Prolog STA s44, bypasses text-match classifiers).\nThe reward signal determines hacking \\emph{composition} but not \\emph{prevalence}: total conclusion-in-premise rates are comparable across ISR and random (${\\sim}82$\\% pooled), but ISR converts H1 into H7.\nSFT remains the only condition with near-zero hacking (92.9\\% genuine), confirming that hacking is RL-reward-specific (Figure~\\ref{fig:hacking-composition}).\n","structuredPatch":[{"oldStart":91,"oldLines":10,"newStart":91,"newLines":9,"lines":[" \\label{sec:crosslogic}"," "," We replicated both experiments using SWI-Prolog 8.4.2 (full results in Appendix Table~\\ref{tab:prolog-crosslogic}).","-Two of three Prolog exec-only seeds yield proved-only ISR near zero (s42${=}0.037$, s43${=}0.015$), confirming diagnostic transfer despite moderate-to-high PR; here low ISR reflects \\emph{redundant coding}---repeated ground facts where removing any single copy leaves the proof intact---rather than absence of reasoning, contrasting with the metric-gaming high ISR under Prolog STA reward.","-With ISR added as a Prolog reward, all three STA seeds converge to gaming with 0\\% genuine across 185 proved samples (universal Goodhart, stronger than the FOL ${\\sim}16$\\% genuine residual): s42 produces clean H7 tautologies (50/50, ISR$^*{=}1.000$); s43 generates uppercase pseudo-Prolog that bypasses text-match classifiers but is functionally H7 ($n_{\\text{nec}}{=}1$ for 38/38); s44 develops query-as-fact gaming where NL is converted to snake\\_case atoms used as both fact and query (ISR$^*{=}0.912$, $n_{\\text{nec}}{=}1$ for 95/97).","-Simpler formal semantics enable more complete exploitation, and the bypass of text-match classifiers by query-as-fact gaming motivates functional cross-validation of automated diagnostics.","-The diversity of gaming pathways across seeds---despite identical training data and reward function---demonstrates that Goodhart's Law in neurosymbolic reasoning is not a single failure mode but a family of convergent exploits.","+Two of three Prolog exec-only seeds yield proved-only ISR near zero (s42${=}0.037$, s43${=}0.015$), confirming diagnostic transfer despite moderate-to-high PR; low ISR here reflects \\emph{redundant coding} (repeated ground facts where removing one leaves the proof intact), not absence of reasoning---contrasting with the metric-gaming high ISR under Prolog STA reward.","+With ISR added as a Prolog reward, all three STA seeds converge to gaming with 0\\% genuine across 185 proved samples (universal Goodhart, stronger than the FOL ${\\sim}16$\\% residual): s42 produces clean H7 tautologies (ISR$^*{=}1.000$); s43 generates uppercase pseudo-Prolog functionally equivalent to H7 ($n_{\\text{nec}}{=}1$ for 38/38); s44 develops query-as-fact gaming, converting NL to snake\\_case atoms used as both fact and query (ISR$^*{=}0.912$, $n_{\\text{nec}}{=}1$ for 95/97).","+The diversity of gaming pathways across seeds---despite identical training data and reward---shows that Goodhart's Law here is not a single failure mode but a family of convergent exploits, and that bypass of text classifiers by query-as-fact gaming motivates functional cross-validation of automated diagnostics."," "," \\subsection{Hacking Taxonomy}"," \\label{sec:taxonomy}"]}],"userModified":false,"replaceAll":false}}
